But we can use theme of "convex-concave procedure": For given p, solve LP for q with p*q ≤ M, sum q =? The Sota LP. We can improve by solving dual of this LP to get subgradient maybe. Let's understand LP.
Given p and M (peak of p*p). Feasible set Q={q≥0: p*q ≤ M, sum q=S?}. Since p is feasible with sum S. The LP maximizes sum q. If optimum q* has sum >S, then rescale q to sum S reduces? The objective F(p)=min{M: p*p≤M, sum=S, p≥0}. The set { (p,M): p≥0,sum=S,p*p≤M } is nonconvex. LP relaxes constraint q*q≤M to p*q≤M (linearization at p). If q* has larger sum, then maybe q has lower F? Because p*q≤M and p feasible, by polarization? For any convex combination r=(1-t)p+tq, r*r = (1-t)^2 p*p +2t(1-t)p*q + t^2 q*q. Algorithm `_step_soft(p, beta, lr, momentum)`:
- b = conv_fast(p) (length 2n-1). Due to FFT symmetry? p nonnegative real, F*F not conjugate? Wait convolution in time domain corresponds to product of DFT with itself, not |F|^2. For linear convolution of real sequences, circular DFT product P*P: at N-ω, P=-ω conjugate, square conjugate? (conj P)^2 = conj(P^2), yes real. Good. Use `F = np.fft.rfft(p, N); b_full = np.fft.irfft(F * F, N)`. This computes circular convolution if N ≥ 2n-1, so linear. Good.
- For soft weights, softmax over b: maxb = b.max(); w = exp(beta*(b - maxb)); w /= w.sum(). If beta large, w may underflow except peaks; okay. If all b near 0? no.

Pseudo:
```
def next_fast_len(N): use scipy.fft.next_fast_len or manual power of 2.

def conv_full(p):
    n=len(p); N=next_fast_len(2*n-1)
    F=np.fft.rfft(p,N); return np.fft.irfft(F*F,N)[:2*n-1]

def corr_weights(p,w):
    n=len(p); N=next_fast_len(3*n-2)
    Fw=np.fft.rfft(w,N); Fp=np.fft.rfft(p[::-1],N)
    h=np.fft.irfft(Fw*Fp,N)
    return 2*h[n-1:n-1+n]
```
Need define functions top-level no closures.

Soft objective:
```
def soft_obj(p, beta):
    b=conv_full(p)
    return float(np.log(np.sum(np.exp(beta*(b-b.max()))))/beta) # plus max? logsumexp stable: m=b.max(); return m + log(sum(exp(beta*(b-m))))/beta
```
Actually (1/β) logsumexp = m + log(sum exp(β(b-m)))/β.
Gradient uses softmax weights. For line search, compute soft_obj new.

We can optimize p on simplex with sum S. Projected normalized:
```
def step_simplex(p, grad, lr, momentum=None):
    grad = grad - grad.mean()
    cand = p - lr*grad
    cand = np.maximum(cand,0)
    s = cand.sum()
    if s <=0: return p
    return cand * (S/s)
```
But if p has exact zeros, gradient may keep them zero. Could add small noise to maintain positivity. Optimal likely positive? Maybe some zeros? But to be safe, after many steps, if min small, we can mix with uniform: p = (1-ε)p + ε uniform, ε=1e-6, to avoid boundary local minima. Could also random restarts.

Adam with projection:
```
z_t? no.
m_hat, v_hat arrays.
lr schedule.
for it:
  b, M, softObj...
  w=softmax(beta*(b-M))
  grad=corr(w,p)
  grad -= grad.mean()
  # Optional: clip grad to avoid outliers
  m = 0.9 m +0.1 grad; v=0.99 v+0.01 grad^2
  step = m_hat/(sqrt(v_hat)+1e-8)

  cand = cand.clip(0); if sum 0 add uniform
  cand *= S/cand.sum()
  # line search: try if soft improved
```

Let's detail optimization function.

```
def _normalize_seq(a, S=None):
    a=np.asarray(a,float); a=np.maximum(a,0)
    if S is None: S=np.sqrt(2*len(a))
    s=a.sum()
    if s < 1e-12: a=np.ones_like(a)
    return a*(S/a.sum())
```

Soft objective function top-level:
```
def _soft_value(b, beta):
    if beta <=0: return float(b.max())
    m=b.max()
    # avoid overflow: beta*(b-m) <=0
    z=beta*(b-m)
    # if beta large, exp underflow fine
    s=np.exp(z).sum()
    if s<=0: return m
    return m + np.log(s)/beta
```
This equals (1/β)logsumexp.

Weight function:
```
def _active_weights(b, beta, top_frac):
    L=len(b); m=b.max()
    if top_frac >0:
       K=max(1,int(top_frac*L))
       idx=np.argpartition(b, -K)[-K:]
       w=np.zeros(L); w[idx]=1.0/K
    else:
       w=None
    if beta >0:
       z=beta*(b-m); ex=np.exp(z); s=ex.sum(); sw=ex/s if s>0 else w
       if w is None: return sw
       # mix? Use top uniform if beta high? Maybe alpha = min(1, beta/100)? Actually top_frac used.
       alpha = 1.0 if beta < 50 else 0.5? Could just return 0.5*(sw+w)
       return 0.5*(sw+w)
    return w if w is not None else uniform
```
Maybe simpler stages: for low β top_frac=0; for high β top_frac>0 and beta=0? Use top only. Combination can smooth.

Gradient correlation:
```
def _grad_corr(p, w):
    n=len(p)
    N=next_fast_len(3*n-2)
    Fw=np.fft.rfft(w,N)
    Fp=np.fft.rfft(p[::-1],N)
    h=np.fft.irfft(Fw*Fp,N)
    return 2.0*h[n-1:n-1+n]
```
Potential overflow in FFT if p large? p sum ~√(2n) ~90 for n=4096, values maybe <10, fine.

Conv full:
```
def _conv_full(p):
    n=len(p)
    N=next_fast_len(2*n-1)
    F=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
    b=np.fft.irfft(F*F,N)
    return b[:2*n-1]
```

Let's define `_project_simplex(p, S)`:
```
p=np.maximum(p,0)
s=p.sum()
if s<=1e-12: p=np.full(len(p), S/len(p))
else: p=p*S/s
return p
```

Adam update function no closure:
```
def _adam_step(p, grad, m, v, t, beta1, beta2, base_lr):
    S=np.sqrt(2*len(p))
    grad = grad - grad.mean()
    m = beta1*m + (1-beta1)*grad
    v = beta2*v + (1-beta2)*grad*grad
    mhat = m/(1-beta1**(t+1))
    vhat = v/(1-beta2**(t+1))
    scale = S / np.sqrt(len(p))
    cand = p - base_lr * scale * mhat/(np.sqrt(vhat)+1e-8)
    return _project_simplex(cand,S), m, v, t+1
```
But `base_lr` absolute? scale ~1.4. Good.

Weights top:
```
def _make_weights(b, beta, top_frac):
    L=len(b)
    wtop=None
    if top_frac > 0.0:
        K=max(1,int(round(top_frac*L)))
        # Use partial argmax; if K==L uniform
        if K >= L:
            wtop=np.full(L,1.0/L)
        else:
            idx=np.argpartition(b, L-K)[-K:]  # top K
            wtop=np.zeros(L); wtop[idx]=1.0/K
    if beta > 0:
        m=b.max(); z=beta*(b-m); ex=np.exp(z); s=ex.sum()
        if s>0: wsk=ex/s
        else: wsk=wtop if wtop is not None else np.full(L,1/L)
        if wtop is None: return wsk
        return 0.5*wsk + 0.5*wtop
    if wtop is not None: return wtop
    return np.full(L,1.0/L)
```
For beta=0 top_frac must >0; if both 0 (not) uniform.

Hard max gradient: if top_frac very small, top K uniform. For true max, K=1. We can have stage top_frac=0.001, K~8. Good.

Adam update code top-level:
```
def _adam_direction(g, m, v, t, b1=0.7, b2=0.95):
    m = b1*m + (1-b1)*g
    v = b2*v + (1-b2)*(g*g)
    mhat = m/(1.0 - b1**(t+1))
    vhat = v/(1.0 - b2**(t+1))
    return mhat/(np.sqrt(vhat)+1e-8), m, v, t+1
```
Using b1=0.7, b2=0.95 for faster forgetting. Step size `base_lr * S/np.sqrt(n)`.

Python overhead maybe more but 2400s enough. Let's do line search every iteration with up to 2 factors. This will be robust. For n=4096, 8000*2 conv = maybe 300s, fine. Plus gradient conv = one more per iter? Wait gradient correlation uses FFT Ncorr (16384) similar cost to conv. Total per iter: current b conv (if not stored) + candidate b conv + gradient corr. If factor rejected and try second, up to two candidate conv. ~2-3 FFTs per iter. 8000 iters *3*0.01=240s. Good.

Pseudo `_optimize_fft(p0, deadline, rng, max_stages=None, label='')`:
```
n=len(p0); S=...
p=_normalize_to_sum(p0,S)
b=_conv_fast(p)
best_score=_score_from_b(b,n,S); best=p.copy()
m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); t=0; lr_mult=1.0; fail=0
stages=[...]
for beta,top_frac,base_lr,iters in stages:
  if len(stages)>? break
  lr=base_lr*lr_mult*S/np.sqrt(n)
  for it in range(iters):
    if time.monotonic() > deadline-2: break
    if fail > 40: perturb reset
    w=_make_weights(b,beta,top_frac)
    g=_grad_corr(p,w); g-=g.mean()
    dir,mn,vn,tn=_adam_direction(g,m,v,t)
    # try factors
    accepted=False
    for factor in [1.0,0.5,0.25]:
       cand=_project_sum(p + factor*lr*dir, S) # sign: dir is mhat/sqrt(v) not negative? Our _adam_direction returns positive gradient normalized; we need step = -lr*dir.
       cb=_conv_fast(cand)
       cscore=_score_from_b(cb,n,S)
       if beta>0:
          csoft=_soft_value(cb,beta); cursoft=_soft_value(b,beta)
          ok = csoft <= cursoft + 1e-12 and cscore <= cur_score*1.05 + 0.02
       else:
          ok = cscore <= cur_score + 1e-12
       if ok:
          p=cand; b=cb; cur_score=cscore; m=mn; v=vn; t=tn; lr_mult=1.0; fail=0; accepted=True; break
    if not accepted:
       # maybe reduce and try no update
       lr_mult=max(0.1, lr_mult*0.7)
       fail+=1
       if fail>50: perturb reset
    # update best if cur_score<best_score
    if cur_score < best_score - 1e-9: best=p.copy(); best_score=cur_score
    if time left < 60 break outer? can't.
```

LP refine function:
```
def _lp_refine(p, deadline, max_iters=20):
    n=len(p); S=sum
    M=max conv
    for it in range(max_iters):
      if time > deadline-10: break
      q=_lp_solve(p,M)
      if q is None: break
      # Try blends
      improved=False
      for t in [0.7,0.5,0.3,0.2,0.1]:
         p2=_normalize_to_sum((1-t)*p + t*q, S)
         s2=_score_fast(p2)
         if s2 < M? or < score_best: accept
      if not improved: break
      M=score
    return best
```
Need `_lp_solve` top-level. Use dense matrix. For n>1024 skip to save memory. But maybe use sparse? `scipy.optimize.linprog` accepts sparse A_ub. Build sparse `scipy.sparse.csr_matrix`. Could handle 2048 if memory. Use sparse to be safe.
```
def _lp_solve(f, M, nlimit=1200):
    if n>nlimit: return None
    rows=[]; cols=[]; vals=[]
    for k in range(2*n-1):
       lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(n-1,k)
       idx=np.arange(lo,hi+1)
       coeffs=f[k-idx]
       if np.any(coeffs): rows.extend([k]*len(idx)); cols.extend(idx.tolist()); vals.extend(coeffs.tolist())
    A=sp.csr_matrix((vals,(rows,cols)),shape=(2*n-1,n))
    res=linprog(-np.ones(n), A_ub=A, b_ub=np.full(2*n-1,M), bounds=[(0,None)]*n, method='highs')
```
Creating lists length ~n^2/2 for n=1024 ~500k okay. For 2048 ~2M, memory okay maybe 50MB; LP solve can be slow. Limit 1024.

LP `M` should be current peak. Since p sum S, feasible. Could add an upper bound on sum to prevent huge? Not necessary; constraints finite. But with f small at edges, q might blow? For each k, A row has f coefficients; max f maybe O(1), so q bounded by M/min(f) at endpoints. f could be tiny at endpoints, making q huge and LP unbounded? Row k=0 coefficient f0. If f0=0 (or zero due clipping), row0 all zeros? If f0=0, constraint 0≤M no bound on q0? Row1 coeff f1 etc. If initial f has zero endpoints, LP might be unbounded for q0? But p has maybe tiny positive? If exact zero, A first rows zero -> unbounded. We can avoid LP if min(f) <=1e-8? Or add small eps to f? The LP as previous with f nonnegative; if f has zeros, max sum q can be unbounded if there is a direction q in nullspace of A nonnegative. For safety, for LP add epsilon to f: `f_lp = f.copy(); f_lp += 1e-6*f.max()` to ensure all coefficients positive? But changing f changes constraints. It's just a heuristic; okay. Also bound variables? Add max q maybe 10*S/n? Could solve finite. Add bounds `(0, 4*S/n*10?)`? If optimal needs larger, not. We can set upper bound `10*S` (sum) to keep finite. Since we only blend, extreme huge not useful. Use bounds list `(0.0, 4.0*S)`? That is per variable huge but finite. Better `upper = 4.0 * S` ensures total not insane. Use `bounds=[(0,10*S)]*n`.

But linprog with HHS and bounds okay.

LP objective max sum q. If unbounded due zeros, bounds save. But with upper bound, feasible. Use `upper = 8.0 * S / max(n,1)`? No, a coordinate could be > average by factor; maybe 10*avg enough? Optimal q could have spike; but blend. Use upper = 10*S (very high). For n=1024 S=45, upper450; finite.

Detailed helper code:

```
import numpy as np
import time
import scipy.sparse as sp
from scipy.optimize import linprog

def _fast_len(N):
    # power of two? scipy next_fast_len may be available
    try:
        from scipy.fft import next_fast_len
        return int(next_fast_len(N))
    except Exception:
        p=1
        while p<N: p <<=1
        return p
```
Import inside function each call? Better top-level import `from scipy.fft import next_fast_len` maybe. Use next_fast_len. If not, fallback? We can import at top. SciPy likely has. Use:
```
from scipy.fft import next_fast_len
```
But older? Use try.

`_conv_fast(p)` uses `N=next_fast_len(2*len(p)-1)`. If p not contiguous? okay.

`_grad_corr(p,w)` uses Ncorr=next_fast_len(3*n-2). `pr = p[::-1]`. Maybe for n=0 no.

`_project_sum(a,S)`:
```
a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
s=a.sum()
if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 1e-300:
    return np.full_like(a, S/a.size)
return a*(S/s)
```

`_score_from_b(b,n,S)`:
```
m=float(np.max(b)) if b.size else 0
if m <0: m=0
s=float(S)
if s<0.01: return inf
return float(2.0*n*m/(s*s))
```
If S fixed √(2n), okay.

`_score_fast(p)`.

`_soft_value(b,beta)`:
```
if beta <=0: return b.max()
m=float(b.max())
z=beta*(b-m)
# np.exp(z) may sum 0? z max0
s=float(np.exp(z).sum())
if s <=0: return m
return m + float(np.log(s))/beta
```
Since b max subtracted, exp(0)=1, s≥1.

`_top_weights(b,K?)` could integrate.

`_make_weights(b,beta,top_frac)` as above. To save repeated b.max, compute.

`_adam_direction(g,m,v,t,b1=0.7,b2=0.95)`:
```
m = b1*m + (1-b1)*g
v = b2*v + (1-b2)*(g*g)
mhat = m / (1.0 - b1**(t+1))
vhat = v / (1.0 - b2**(t+1))
step = mhat / (np.sqrt(vhat) + 1e-8)
return step, m,v,t+1
```

`_perturb(p,rng,amp)`:
```
noise = rng.standard_normal(len(p))*amp
return _project_sum(p+noise, p.sum())
```

`_optimize_fft` with stages. Need pass `max_iters_per_stage` maybe scaled by n. Let's define base stage times. Since time budget dynamic, we can simply run until deadline through stages; no hard max except time.

But if stages total iters too high, time breaks. Good.

Set stages:
```
STAGES = [
 (2.0, 0.0, 0.12, 400),
 (5.0, 0.0, 0.08, 500),
 (12.0, 0.0, 0.05, 600),
 (30.0, 0.015, 0.03, 800),
 (80.0, 0.04, 0.018, 1000),
 (0.0, 0.08, 0.010, 1200),
 (0.0, 0.16, 0.005, 1500),

`_interpolate_sequence`:
```
def _interpolate_sequence(a,n):
   a=np.asarray(a,float); m=len(a)
   if m==0: return np.ones(n)
   if m==n: return a.copy()
   if m==1: return np.full(n,a[0])
   xp=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,m); fp=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
   return np.interp(fp,xp,a)
```

```
def _smooth_random(n,rng):
    x=rng.standard_normal(n)
    # simple moving average
    k=max(1,n//32)
    kernel=np.ones(k)/k
    y=np.convolve(x,kernel,mode='same')
    a=np.abs(y)+0.1
    return a
```
But np.convolve O(n*k) fine. Or FFT lowpass.

def f1: x**rng.uniform(0.2,2)
def f2: (1-x)**...
def f3: x**a*(1-x)**b
```

def _lp_polish(p, deadline, max_iters=8):
    n=len(p); S=float(p.sum())
    b=_conv_fast(p); score=_score_from_b(b,n,S); best=p.copy(); best_score=score
    M=float(np.max(b))
    for it in range(max_iters):
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 5: break
       q=_lp_solve(p,M,n)
       if q is None: break
       q=_project_sum(q,S) # maybe
       improved=False
       for t in (0.8,0.5,0.3,0.2,0.1):
          p2=_project_sum((1-t)*p + t*q,S)
          s2=_score_fast(p2)
          if s2 < best_score - 1e-9:
             p=p2; best=p2.copy(); best_score=s2; M=float(_conv_fast(p2).max()); improved=True; break
       if not improved:
          # try tiny gradient step? Maybe not
          break
    return best,best_score
```

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    t_start=time.monotonic()
    total_budget=float(budget_s)-10
    deadline=t_start+total_budget
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    best_seq=None; best_score=inf
    prev = globals maybe
    if prev size>0: seq0=prev; else seq0=rng.rand(512)
    # Ensure length maybe use 512. If prev length !=512, use it if <=512? Use min.
    # Work lengths:
    lengths = [512,1024,2048,4096]
    # If budget small, lengths adjust
    # We'll maintain current_best_small for interpolation.
    current_start = None
    for idx,n in enumerate(lengths):
       if time > deadline - 30: break
       alloc_start = t_start + total_budget * idx/len(lengths)
       alloc_end = t_start + total_budget * (idx+1)/len(lengths)
       # Extra time for last? If final, use deadline.
       if idx == len(lengths)-1: alloc_end=deadline
       # if previous small exists, interpolate; else random
       if current_start is not None and len(current_start)>0:
           base=_interpolate_sequence(current_start,n)
       else: base=seq0
       starts=_make_starts(n,rng,base, prev)
       # allocate: top start 0.8 of slice, second 0.2
       starts = sorted by score
       for j,a0 in enumerate(starts[:2]):
           sl=alloc_end-alloc_start
           if j==0: dl=alloc_start + 0.75*sl
           else: dl=alloc_end - 5
           if time > dl - 20: break
           seq, score=_optimize_fft(a0, dl, rng)
           if score < best_score: update; current_start=seq
       # LP polish best current if n<=768 and time before next
       if n<=768 and time < alloc_end-20:
            if best_seq is not None and len(best_seq)==n:
                seq, score=_lp_polish(best_seq, alloc_end-10, 6)
                update
    # final polish best at its length if time
    if best_seq is not None and time < deadline-10:
        seq, score=_optimize_fft(best_seq, deadline-5, rng, maybe fewer stages?)
        update
    out = _project_sum(best_seq, 1.0) if best_seq is not None else _project_sum(np.ones(512),1.0)
    # final eval validate
    print...
    return [float(x) for x in out]
```

Let's detail `_make_starts`:
```
def _make_starts(n, rng, base, prev_seq):
   S=np.sqrt(2.0*n)
   cand=[_normalize_to_sum(base,S)]
   # noisy base
   cand.append(_normalize_to_sum(base + rng.normal(0,0.05/base.std()? , n), S))
   # smooth random
   cand.append(_smooth_random(n,rng))
   cand.append(_sinusoid_start(n,rng))
   cand.append(_power_start(n,rng))
   # previous if not base
   if prev_seq is not None: cand.append(_interpolate_sequence(prev_seq,n))
```
Need avoid division by zero. For noise base: standard deviation `sigma = max(1e-6, 0.05*float(np.mean(base)))`. Use `np.mean`.

`_smooth_random`:
```
def _smooth_random(n,rng):
   x=rng.standard_normal(n)
   k=max(1, int(max(3, n//24)))
   kernel=np.ones(k)/k
   y=np.convolve(x,kernel,mode='same')
   a=np.abs(y)+0.2
   a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
   return a
```
`_sinusoid_start`:
```
x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
nu=rng.integers(1,6)
ph1=rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi); ph2=...
a=0.6 + 0.4*np.sin(2*np.pi*nu*x+ph1) + 0.3*np.cos(2*np.pi*(nu+1)*x+ph2)
return np.maximum(a,0.0)
```
`_power_start`:
```
a1=rng.uniform(0.1,3.0); b1=rng.uniform(0.1,3.0)
x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
a=x**a1 * (1-np.clip(x,0,1))**b1
a=a/max(a.max(),1e-6)+0.1
# maybe asym: flip with probability
if rng.random()<0.5: a=a[::-1]
return a
```
Use clipped x for 1-x; avoid negative due floating: `x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)`.

`_normalize_to_sum` if S parameter None? We'll pass S. top-level:
```
def _normalize_to_sum(a, S):
   a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
   if a.ndim !=1: a=a.ravel()
   a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
   a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
   s=float(a.sum())
   if s < 1e-300:
      if a.size==0: a=np.ones(1)
      a=np.full(a.size, max(0.0,S/a.size if a.size>0 else 1.0))
   else:
      a = a*(S/s)
   return a
```
If a.size 0, return ones? For n>0.

`_project_sum` essentially same.

`_lp_polish`:
```
def _lp_polish(p, deadline, max_iters=8, max_n=768):
    if len(p)>max_n: return p,float('inf')
    n=len(p); S=float(p.sum())
    b=_conv_fast(p); cur_score=_score_from_b(b,n,S); best=p.copy(); best_score=cur_score
    M=float(np.max(b))
    for it in range(max_iters):
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 5: break
       q=_lp_solve(p,M,max_n)
       if q is None: break
       q=_normalize_to_sum(q,S)
       improved=False
       for t in (0.8,0.5,0.3,0.2,0.1):
           p2=_normalize_to_sum((1.0-t)*p + t*q, S)
           b2=_conv_fast(p2); s2=_score_from_b(b2,n,S)
           if s2 < best_score - 1e-9:
               p=p2; b=b2; cur_score=s2; best=p.copy(); best_score=s2; M=float(np.max(b)); improved=True; break
       if not improved:
           break
    return best,best_score
```
In LP, `M` should be current peak of p with sum S. Since constraints f*q≤M. Good.

Detailed code for `_optimize_fft`:
```
def _optimize_fft(p0, deadline, rng):
    n=len(p0); S=np.sqrt(2.0*n)
    p=_normalize_to_sum(p0,S)
    b=_conv_fast(p)
    cur_score=_score_from_b(b,n,S)
    best=p.copy(); best_score=cur_score
    m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); t=0; lr_mult=1.0; fail=0
    for beta, top_frac, base_lr, iters in _STAGES:
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 2.0: break
       cur_soft = _soft_value(b, beta) if beta>0 else None
       it=0
       for it in range(iters):
           if time.monotonic() > deadline - 2.0: break
           w=_make_weights(b,beta,top_frac)
           g=_grad_corr(p,w)
           g = g - g.mean()
           gnorm=float(np.linalg.norm(g))
           if gnorm < 1e-12:
               break
           dirn, m2, v2, t2 = _adam_direction(g, m, v, t)
           step = base_lr * lr_mult * (S/np.sqrt(n))
           # try factors
           accepted=False
           last_cand=None; last_cb=None; last_cs=None; last_cs_soft=None
           for factor in (1.0, 0.45, 0.2):
               cand=_project_sum(p - factor*step*dirn, S)
               cb=_conv_fast(cand)
               cs=_score_from_b(cb,n,S)
               if beta > 0:
                   cs_soft=_soft_value(cb,beta)
                   ok = cs_soft <= cur_soft + 1e-10
                   if ok and cs > cur_score + max(0.02, 0.05*cur_score):
                       ok=False
               else:
                   ok = cs <= cur_score + 1e-10
               if ok:
                   p=cand; b=cb; cur_score=cs; m=m2; v=v2; t=t2; lr_mult=1.0; fail=0
                   if beta >0: cur_soft=_soft_value(b,beta)
                   accepted=True; break
               else:
                   last_cand=cand; last_cb=cb; last_cs=cs; last_cs_soft=cs_soft if beta>0 else None
           if not accepted:
               # maybe if fail high accept last under guard
               if fail >= 12 and last_cand is not None:
                   if last_cs < best_score*1.25 + 0.2:
                       p=last_cand; b=last_cb; cur_score=last_cs; m=m2; v=v2; t=t2; lr_mult=0.8; fail=0
                       if beta>0: cur_soft=_soft_value(b,beta)
                       accepted=True
               if not accepted:
                   lr_mult = max(0.1, lr_mult*0.75)
                   fail += 1
                   if fail >= 25:
                       # reset to best or perturb
                       if cur_score > best_score*1.15 + 0.05:
                           p=best.copy(); b=_conv_fast(p); cur_score=best_score
                       else:
                           amp=0.07*(S/np.sqrt(n))
                           p=_suggest? _perturb(p,rng,amp)
                           b=_conv_fast(p); cur_score=_score_from_b(b,n,S)
                           if cur_score > best_score*1.2: p=best.copy(); b=_conv_fast(p); cur_score=best_score
                       m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); t=0; fail=0; lr_mult=1.0
                       if beta>0: cur_soft=_soft_value(b,beta)
           if cur_score < best_score - 1e-9:
               best=p.copy(); best_score=cur_score
           # occasional print? no, maybe at stage start? Not.
       # after stage, maybe reset lr_mult
       lr_mult=1.0; fail=0
    return best,best_score
```

Can we modify this construction analytically to get better? This is huge! Let's explore.

Q(z) truncated (1-z)^(-1/2) exactly yields Q^2 coefficients: for k<n, 1; for k≥n, 1 - ∑_{i=n}^{2n-2-k?} etc. The maximum is exactly 1 (for k<n). Thus it is feasible with Q^2≤1. def _harmonicond_start(n):
   c=np.empty(n); c[0]=1.0
   for i in range(1,n): c[i]=c[i-1]*(2.0*i-1.0)/(2.0*i)
   return c
```

If scipy.fft not available? It is. Could try except.

```
def _fft_len(n):
    try:
        return int(next_fast_len(n))
    except Exception:
        p=1
        while p<n: p <<=1
        return p
```

In `_conv_fast`, if n=0? not.

```
def _conv_fast(p):
    p=np.asarray(p,dtype=np.float64)
    n=p.size
    N=_fft_len(2*n-1)
    F=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
    b=np.fft.irfft(F*F,N)[:2*n-1]
    if b.size:
       b=np.maximum(b,0.0)
    return b
```
Multiplying F*F not conj; correct for convolution of same sequence. If p real but F complex, product can have small imaginary; irfft assumes real input with Hermitian symmetry. Is F*F Hermitian? For real p, F[N-k]=conj(F[k]); (F[N-k])^2 = conj(F[k])^2 = conj(F[k]^2). Good. Tiny imaginary from rounding okay.

`_grad_corr`:
```
def _grad_corr(p,w):
    n=p.size
    N=_fft_len(3*n-2)
    Fw=np.fft.rfft(w,N)
    Fp=np.fft.rfft(p[::-1],N)
    h=np.fft.irfft(Fw*Fp,N)
    return 2.0*h[n-1:n-1+n]
```
For n=1, 3n-2=1. okay.

`_soft_value`: if beta small maybe `np.log(s)` s≥1. okay.

`_perturb`:
```
def _perturb(p,rng,amp):
    noise=rng.standard_normal(p.size)*amp
    return _project_sum(p+noise, float(p.sum()))
```

`_make_starts` returns list arrays sorted by score. It uses `_harmononic_start`.

```
def _harmononic_start(n):
    c=np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    c[0]=1.0
    for i in range(1,n):
       c[i]=c[i-1]*((2.0*i-1.0)/(2.0*i))
    return c
```

`_make_starts`:
```
def _make_starts(n, rng, base, prev):
    S=np.sqrt(2.0*n)
    cand=[]
    if base is not None and base.size>0:
       cand.append(_normalize_to_sum(base,S))
    if prev is not None and prev.size>0:
       cand.append(_normalize_to_sum(_interpolate_sequence(prev,n),S))
    cand.append(_normalize_to_sum(_harmononic_start(n),S))
    cand.append(_normalize_to_sum(_harmononic_start(n)[::-1],S))
    # random shape w
    x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    for _ in range(2):
       a=rng.uniform(-0.5,1.0); b=rng.uniform(-0.3,0.8)
       w=1.0 + a*x + b*x*x
       w=np.maximum(w,0.05)
       q=_harmononic_start(n)*w
       if rng.random()<0.5: q=q[::-1]
       cand.append(_normalize_to_sum(q,S))
    # smooth random
    cand.append(_normalize_to_sum(_smooth_random(n,rng),S))
    # sinusoid
    cand.append(_normalize_to_sum(_sinusoid_start(n,rng),S))
    # score and unique
```
Compute q each time; `_harmononic_start` recomputed multiple times. For efficiency, compute once in function:
```
c=_harmononic_start(n); c_rev=c[::-1].copy()
```

def _power_start(n,rng):
   x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   a=rng.uniform(0.2,3.0); b=rng.uniform(0.2,3.0)
   y=np.power(x,a) * np.power(1-x,b)
   if rng.random()<0.5: y=y[::-1]
   return np.maximum(y+0.1,0.0)

Implement `_tail_refine` quickly:
```
def _tail_refine(p, deadline, rng):
    n=len(p); S=np.sqrt(2.0*n)
    p=_normalize_to_sum(p,S)
    b=_conv_fast(p); cur_score=_score_from_b(b,n,S); best=p.copy(); best_score=cur_score
    m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); t=0; fail=0
    top_frac=0.12; lr_mult=1.0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 2.0:
       w=_make_weights(b,0.0,top_frac)
       g=_grad_corr(p,w); g=g-g.mean(); gnorm=np.linalg.norm(g)
       if gnorm <1e-12: break
       dirn,m2,v2,t2=_adam_direction(g,m,v,t)
       step=0.004*lr_mult*S/np.sqrt(n)
       accepted=False
       last_cand=None; last_b=None; last_s=None
       for factor in (1.0,0.5):
          cand=_project_sum(p - factor*step*dirn,S)
          cb=_conv_fast(cand); cs=_score_from_b(cb,n,S)
          if cs <= cur_score + 1e-12:
             p=cand;b=cb;cur_score=cs;m=m2;v=v2;t=t2;lr_mult=1;fail=0;accepted=True;break
          last_cand=cand; last_b=cb; last_s=cs
       if not accepted:
          if fail >=10 and last_cand is not None and last_s < best_score*1.2 + 0.2:
             p=last_cand;b=last_b;cur_score=last_s;m=m2;v=v2;t=t2;fail=0;lr_mult=1
          else:
             lr_mult=max(0.2,lr_mult*0.7); fail+=1
             if fail>=25:
                p=best.copy(); b=_conv_fast(p); cur_score=best_score; m=zeros; v=zeros; t=0; lr_mult=1; fail=0
       if cur_score < best_score: best=p.copy(); best_score=cur_score
    return best,best_score
```
Use final residual. Good.

In propose final:
```

   seq,score=_tail_refine(best_seq, deadline-2, rng)
   update
```

Pseudo final code for `_optimize_fft`:
```
def _optimize_fft(p0, deadline, rng):
    n = len(p0)
    S = np.sqrt(2.0 * n)
    p = _normalize_to_sum(p0, S)
    b = _conv_fast(p)
    cur_score = _score_from_b(b, n, S)
    best = p.copy(); best_score = cur_score
    m = np.zeros(n); v = np.zeros(n); t = 0; lr_mult = 1.0; fail=0
    for beta, top_frac, base_lr, iters in _STAGES:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 2.0:
            break
        cur_soft = _soft_value(b, beta) if beta > 0.0 else None
        for it in range(iters):
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - 2.0:
                break
            w = _make_weights(b, beta, top_frac)
            g = _grad_corr(p, w)
            g = g - g.mean()
            gnorm = float(np.linalg.norm(g))
            if gnorm <= 1e-12:
                break
            dirn, m2, v2, t2 = _adam_direction(g, m, v, t)
            step = base_lr * lr_mult * (S / np.sqrt(n))
            accepted = False
            last_cand = None; last_cb = None; last_cs = None
            for factor in (1.0, 0.45, 0.2):
                cand = _project_sum(p - factor * step * dirn, S)
                cb = _conv_fast(cand)
                cs = _score_from_b(cb, n, S)
                if beta > 0.0:
                    cs_soft = _soft_value(cb, beta)
                    ok = cs_soft <= cur_soft + 1e-10
                    if ok and cs > cur_score + max(0.02, 0.05 * cur_score):
                        ok = False
                else:
                    ok = cs <= cur_score + 1e-10
                if ok:
                    p = cand; b = cb; cur_score = cs
                    m = m2; v = v2; t = t2; lr_mult = 1.0; fail = 0
                    if beta > 0.0:
                        cur_soft = _soft_value(b, beta)
                    accepted = True
                    break
                else:
                    last_cand = cand; last_cb = cb; last_cs = cs
            if not accepted:
                if fail >= 10 and last_cand is not None:
                    if last_cs < best_score * 1.25 + 0.2:
                        p = last_cand; b = last_cb; cur_score = last_cs
                        m = m2; v = v2; t = t2; lr_mult = 1.0; fail = 0
                        if beta > 0.0:
                            cur_soft = _soft_value(b, beta)
                        accepted = True
                if not accepted:
                    lr_mult = max(0.1, lr_mult * 0.75)
                    fail += 1
                    if fail >= 25:
                        if cur_score > best_score + 0.08:
                            p = best.copy(); b = _conv_fast(p); cur_score = best_score
                        else:
                            amp = 0.07 * (S / np.sqrt(n))
                            p = _perturb(p, rng, amp)
                            b = _conv_fast(p)
                            cur_score = _score_from_b(b, n, S)
                            if cur_score > best_score + 0.2:
                                p = best.copy(); b = _conv_fast(p); cur_score = best_score
                        m = np.zeros(n); v = np.zeros(n); t = 0
                        lr_mult = 1.0; fail = 0
                        if beta > 0.0:
                            cur_soft = _soft_value(b, beta)
            if cur_score < best_score - 1e-9:
                best_score = cur_score; best = p.copy()
        # stage end? maybe print not
    return best, best_score
```

Use dense LP. Build A each iteration O(n^2) ~0.5M for 700, fine. `linprog` with dense. Add upper bound.
```
def _lp_solve(f,M,max_n=700):
  n=len(f)
  if n>max_n or M<=0: return None
  fmax=float(np.max(f))
  if fmax<=1e-12: return None
  fe=f + 1e-10*fmax
  A=np.zeros((2*n-1,n), dtype=np.float64)
  for k in range(2*n-1): ...
  c_obj=-np.ones(n)
  b_ub=np.full(2*n-1, M)
  upper=20.0*float(np.sum(f))
  bounds=[(0.0, upper)]*n
  try: res=linprog(c_obj,A_ub=A,b_ub=b_ub,bounds=bounds,method='highs')
  except: return None
  if res.status==0: return np.maximum(res.x,0)
```
Using `bounds` list of tuples; no lambda. Good.

LP performance: For n=700, dense A 1M; linprog may be seconds. max_iters 3.

```python
"""
Search for a non-negative sequence minimizing
    2*n*max(convolve(a,a)) / (sum(a))^2

The program uses FFT-based projected gradient / Adam-style updates on soft
max approximations of the peak convolution, several multi-scale starts
(up-sampled previous best, fractional-power starts, sinusoidal/power starts),
and a final LP polish for shorter sequences. It adapts to the time budget and
returns the best sequence found.
"""

import time
import numpy as np

try:
    from scipy.fft import next_fast_len as _scipy_next_fast_len
except Exception:
    _scipy_next_fast_len = None

try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog as _scipy_linprog
except Exception:
    _scipy_linprog = None


_FFST_LEN_CACHE = {}


def _fft_len(m):
    """Small helper to obtain a fast FFT length at least as large as m."""
    if m in _FFST_LEN_CACHE:
        return _FFST_LEN_CACHE[m]
    if _scipy_next_fast_len is not None:
        try:
            L = int(_scipy_next_fast_len(m))
            if L >= m:
                _FFST_LEN_CACHE[m] = L
                return L
        except Exception:
            pass
    L = 1
    while L < m:
        L <<= 1
    _FFST_LEN_CACHE[m] = L
    return L


def _normalize_to_sum(a, S):
    """Clip to [0, inf), replace non-finite by zero, and rescale to sum S."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.ndim != 1:
        a = a.ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.full(1, float(S), dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 1e-300:
        a = np.full(a.size, S / a.size, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        a = a * (S / s)
    return a


def _project_sum(a, S):
    """Project onto non-negative vectors with sum S."""
    return _normalize_to_sum(a, S)


def _score_from_b(b, n, S):
    """Objective from convolution array b, length n and sum S."""
    if n <= 0 or b.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    S = float(S)
    if S < 0.01:
        return float("inf")
    m = float(np.max(b))
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m < 0.0:
        m = 0.0
    return float(2.0 * n * m / (S * S))


def _conv_fast(p):
    """Full linear convolution p * p, zero-padded FFT."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    N = _fft_len(2 * n - 1)
    F = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
    b = np.fft.irfft(F * F, N)[: (2 * n - 1)]
    b = np.maximum(b, 0.0)
    return b


def _score_fast(p):
    """Objective estimated by FFT convolution."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    return _score_from_b(_conv_fast(p), p.size, float(p.sum()))


def _gradient_from_weights(p, w):
    """
    Gradient of sum_k w[k] * (p*p)[k] with respect to p.
    conv(p,p) is index-sum convolution, so this is a correlated sum:
        grad[i] = 2 * sum_k w[k] p[k-i]
    """
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    N = _fft_len(3 * n - 2)
    Fw = np.fft.rfft(np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64), N)
    Fp = np.fft.rfft(p[::-1], N)
    h = np.fft.irfft(Fw * Fp, N)
    return 2.0 * h[(n - 1):(n - 1 + n)]


def _soft_value(b, beta):
    """
    Smooth approximation (1/beta) logsumexp(beta*b).
    For beta <= 0 this returns max(b).
    """
    if beta <= 0.0:
        return float(np.max(b)) if b.size else 0.0
    m = float(np.max(b))
    z = beta * (b - m)
    s = float(np.exp(z).sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return m
    return m + float(np.log(s)) / beta


def _make_weights(b, beta, top_frac):
    """
    Combine a softmax weight and a top-peak uniform weight.
    top_frac is a fraction in [0,1] of the convolution coefficients.
    """
    b = np.asarray(b, dtype=np.float64)
    L = b.size
    if L == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)

    wtop = None
    if top_frac > 0.0:
        K = int(round(top_frac * L))
        if K <= 0:
            K = 1
        if K >= L:
            wtop = np.full(L, 1.0 / L, dtype=np.float64)
        else:
            idx = np.argpartition(b, L - K)[(L - K):]
            wtop = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
            wtop[idx] = 1.0 / K

    if beta > 0.0:
        m = float(np.max(b))
        z = beta * (b - m)
        ex = np.exp(z)
        s = float(ex.sum())
        if np.isfinite(s) and s > 0.0:
            wsk = ex / s
        else:
            wsk = wtop if wtop is not None else np.full(L, 1.0 / L, dtype=np.float64)
        if wtop is None:
            return wsk
        return 0.5 * wsk + 0.5 * wtop

    if wtop is not None:
        return wtop
    return np.full(L, 1.0 / L, dtype=np.float64)


def _adam_direction(g, m, v, t, b1=0.8, b2=0.95):
    """
    Adam-like normalized direction from gradient g.
    Returns (direction, new_m, new_v, t+1).
    """
    g = np.asarray(g, dtype=np.float64)
    m = b1 * m + (1.0 - b1) * g
    v = b2 * v + (1.0 - b2) * (g * g)
    mhat = m / (1.0 - (b1 ** (t + 1)))
    vhat = v / (1.0 - (b2 ** (t + 1)))
    dirn = mhat / (np.sqrt(vhat) + 1e-8)
    return dirn, m, v, t + 1


def _perturb(p, rng, amp):
    """Small random Gaussian perturbation, reprojected to nonnegative sum."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    noise = rng.standard_normal(p.size) * float(amp)
    return _project_sum(p + noise, float(p.sum()))


def _harmononic_start(n):
    """
    Construct the first n coefficients of (1 - z)^(-1/2),
    i.e. central binomial coefficients divided by 4^k.
    This is an excellent analytic starting point: the infinite sequence has
    exact convolution coefficients equal to 1 for all k < n.
    """
    c = np.empty(max(1, int(n)), dtype=np.float64)
    c[0] = 1.0
    for i in range(1, c.size):
        c[i] = c[i - 1] * ((2.0 * i - 1.0) / (2.0 * i))
    return c


def _smooth_random(n, rng):
    """Smoothed random nonnegative start."""
    x = rng.standard_normal(n)
    k = max(1, int(n // 32))
    kernel = np.ones(k, dtype=np.float64) / float(k)
    y = np.convolve(x, kernel, mode="same")
    y = np.abs(y) + 0.25
    return np.maximum(y, 0.0)


def _sinusoid_start(n, rng):
    """Asymmetric sinusoidal nonnegative start."""
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    nu1 = int(rng.integers(1, 5))
    nu2 = int(rng.integers(1, 7))
    a1 = rng.uniform(0.25, 0.75)
    a2 = rng.uniform(0.10, 0.45)
    ph1 = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi)
    ph2 = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi)
    y = 0.75 + a1 * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * nu1 * x + ph1) + a2 * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * nu2 * x + ph2)
    return np.maximum(y, 0.0)


def _power_start(n, rng):
    """Power-law shape, possibly reversed, with a positive floor."""
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    a = rng.uniform(0.2, 3.0)
    b = rng.uniform(0.2, 3.0)
    y = np.power(x, a) * np.power(np.maximum(1.0 - x, 0.0), b)
    if rng.random() < 0.5:
        y = y[::-1]
    y = y + 0.1
    return np.maximum(y, 0.0)


def _interpolate_sequence(a, n):
    """Linearly resample an array to length n while preserving non-negativity."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.ndim != 1:
        a = a.ravel()
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    m = a.size
    if m == 0:
        return np.ones(max(1, int(n)), dtype=np.float64)
    if m == 1:
        return np.full(max(1, int(n)), a[0], dtype=np.float64)
    if m == n:
        return a.copy()
    xp = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m)
    fp = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    return np.maximum(np.interp(fp, xp, a), 0.0)


def _get_global_sequence():
    """Safely read the optional global variable height_sequence_1."""
    seq = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    if seq is None:
        return None
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
        return None
    arr = arr[np.isfinite(arr)]
    arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
    if arr.size == 0:
        return None
    return arr


def _make_starts(n, rng, base, prev):
    """
    Build and sort several starts for length n.
    Returns the best 3 by direct FFT score.
    """
    S = np.sqrt(2.0 * n)
    c = _harmononic_start(n)
    c_rev = c[::-1].copy()

    cand = []

    if base is not None and base.size > 0:
        cand.append(_normalize_to_sum(base, S))

    if prev is not None and prev.size > 0:
        cand.append(_normalize_to_sum(_interpolate_sequence(prev, n), S))

    cand.append(_normalize_to_sum(c, S))
    cand.append(_normalize_to_sum(c_rev, S))

    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    for _ in range(3):
        a = rng.uniform(-0.6, 1.2)
        b = rng.uniform(-0.5, 1.0)
        w = 1.0 + a * x + b * x * x
        w = np.maximum(w, 0.05)
        q = c * w
        if rng.random() < 0.5:
            q = q[::-1]
        cand.append(_normalize_to_sum(q, S))

    cand.append(_normalize_to_sum(_smooth_random(n, rng), S))
    cand.append(_normalize_to_sum(_sinusoid_start(n, rng), S))
    cand.append(_normalize_to_sum(_power_start(n, rng), S))

    scored = []
    idx = 0
    for arr in cand:
        if arr is None:
            continue
        arr = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64)
        if arr.size != n:
            arr = _interpolate_sequence(arr, n)
        arr = _normalize_to_sum(arr, S)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(arr)):
            continue
        b = _conv_fast(arr)
        s = _score_from_b(b, n, S)
        if np.isfinite(s):
            scored.append((s, idx, arr))
            idx += 1

    if not scored:
        return [_normalize_to_sum(c, S)]

    scored.sort()
    out = []
    for _score, _idx, arr in scored:
        if len(out) >= 3:
            break
        out.append(arr.copy())
    return out


def _lp_solve(f, M, max_n=512):
    """
    Sota LP:
        maximize sum(q)
        subject to f*q <= M (elementwise), q >= 0, q <= upper.
    """
    if _scipy_linprog is None:
        return None
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64)
    n = f.size
    if n > max_n or n <= 0 or M <= 0.0:
        return None
    fmax = float(np.max(f))
    if fmax <= 1e-12:
        return None

    # Add a tiny epsilon to avoid unbounded zero rows.
    ff = f + 1e-10 * fmax

    A = np.zeros((2 * n - 1, n), dtype=np.float64)
    for k in range(2 * n - 1):
        lo = max(0, k - n + 1)
        hi = min(n - 1, k)
        # A[k, i] = f[k - i] for i = lo..hi
        A[k, lo:hi + 1] = ff[(k - hi):(k - lo + 1)][::-1]

    c_obj = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    b_ub = np.full(2 * n - 1, float(M), dtype=np.float64)
    upper = 20.0 * float(np.sum(f))
    bounds = [(0.0, upper) for _ in range(n)]

    try:
        res = _scipy_linprog(
            c_obj,
            A_ub=A,
            b_ub=b_ub,
            bounds=bounds,
            method="highs",
        )
    except Exception:
        return None

    if res is not None and res.status == 0:
        q = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
        q = np.where(np.isfinite(q), q, 0.0)
        return np.maximum(q, 0.0)
    return None


def _lp_polish(p, deadline, max_iters=4, max_n=512):
    """
    Optional LP polish for short sequences.
    """
    p = _normalize_to_sum(np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64), np.sqrt(2.0 * len(p)))
    n = len(p)
    S = float(p.sum())

    if n > max_n or time.monotonic() > deadline - 10.0:
        return p.copy(), float("inf")

    b = _conv_fast(p)
    cur_score = _score_from_b(b, n, S)
    best = p.copy()
    best_score = cur_score
    M = float(np.max(b)) if b.size else 0.0

    for _ in range(max_iters):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 5.0:
            break
        q = _lp_solve(p, M, max_n)
        if q is None:
            break
        q = _normalize_to_sum(q, S)

        improved = False
        for t in (0.8, 0.5, 0.3, 0.2, 0.1):
            p2 = _normalize_to_sum((1.0 - t) * p + t * q, S)
            b2 = _conv_fast(p2)
            s2 = _score_from_b(b2, n, S)
            if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < best_score - 1e-10:
                p = p2
                b = b2
                cur_score = s2
                best = p2.copy()
                best_score = s2
                M = float(np.max(b)) if b.size else 0.0
                improved = True
                break

        if not improved:
            break

    return best, best_score


# Stages: (beta, top_frac, base_lr, iters)
# (beta > 0) uses softmax; (beta = 0) uses pure top-peak subgradient.
_STAGES = [
    (1.0, 0.00, 0.18, 220),
    (3.0, 0.00, 0.12, 320),
    (8.0, 0.00, 0.08, 420),
    (20.0, 0.01, 0.05, 520),
    (50.0, 0.02, 0.03, 700),
    (150.0, 0.05, 0.018, 900),
    (0.0, 0.10, 0.010, 1200),
    (0.0, 0.20, 0.005, 1500),
    (0.0, 0.35, 0.0025, 1800),
    (0.0, 0.50, 0.0012, 1000000),
]


def _optimize_fft(p0, deadline, rng):
    """
    FFT-based local optimization.
    Maintains non-negativity and fixed sum sqrt(2n).
    """
    p0 = np.asarray(p0, dtype=np.float64)
    n = p0.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64), float("inf")

    S = np.sqrt(2.0 * float(n))
    p = _normalize_to_sum(p0, S)
    b = _conv_fast(p)
    cur_score = _score_from_b(b, n, S)

    best = p.copy()
    best_score = cur_score

    m = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    t = 0
    lr_mult = 1.0
    fail = 0

    for beta, top_frac, base_lr, iters in _STAGES:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 2.0:
            break

        cur_soft = _soft_value(b, beta) if beta > 0.0 else None

        for _it in range(iters):
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - 2.0:
                break

            w = _make_weights(b, beta, top_frac)
            g = _gradient_from_weights(p, w)
            g = g - g.mean()
            gnorm = float(np.linalg.norm(g))
            if gnorm <= 1e-12:
                break

            dirn, m2, v2, t2 = _adam_direction(g, m, v, t)
            step = base_lr * lr_mult * (S / np.sqrt(float(n)))

            accepted = False
            last_cand = None
            last_cb = None
            last_cs = None

            for factor in (1.0, 0.45, 0.20):
                cand = _project_sum(p - factor * step * dirn, S)
                cb = _conv_fast(cand)
                cs = _score_from_b(cb, n, S)

                if beta > 0.0:
                    cs_soft = _soft_value(cb, beta)
                    ok = cs_soft <= cur_soft + 1e-10
                    # prevent excessively bad true objective while using soft
                    if ok and cs > cur_score + max(0.03, 0.10 * cur_score):
                        ok = False
                else:
                    ok = cs <= cur_score + 1e-10

                if ok:
                    p = cand
                    b = cb
                    cur_score = cs
                    m = m2
                    v = v2
                    t = t2
                    lr_mult = 1.0
                    fail = 0
                    if beta > 0.0:
                        cur_soft = _soft_value(b, beta)
                    accepted = True
                    break
                else:
                    last_cand = cand
                    last_cb = cb
                    last_cs = cs

            if not accepted:
                # Occasionally accept a small worsening to escape very flat local optima.
                if fail >= 10 and last_cand is not None and last_cs < best_score * 1.25 + 0.25:
                    p = last_cand
                    b = last_cb
                    cur_score = last_cs
                    m = m2
                    v = v2
                    t = t2
                    lr_mult = 1.0
                    fail = 0
                    if beta > 0.0:
                        cur_soft = _soft_value(b, beta)
                    accepted = True

                if not accepted:
                    lr_mult = max(0.1, lr_mult * 0.75)
                    fail += 1

                    if fail >= 25:
                        if cur_score > best_score + 0.08:
                            p = best.copy()
                            b = _conv_fast(p)
                            cur_score = best_score
                        else:
                            amp = 0.07 * (S / np.sqrt(float(n)))
                            p = _perturb(p, rng, amp)
                            b = _conv_fast(p)
                            cur_score = _score_from_b(b, n, S)
                            if cur_score > best_score + 0.25:
                                p = best.copy()
                                b = _conv_fast(p)
                                cur_score = best_score

                        m = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
                        v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
                        t = 0
                        lr_mult = 1.0
                        fail = 0
                        if beta > 0.0:
                            cur_soft = _soft_value(b, beta)

            if cur_score < best_score - 1e-10:
                best_score = cur_score
                best = p.copy()

    # Make sure the returned best is valid.
    best = _project_sum(best, S)
    return best, best_score


def _tail_refine(p, deadline, rng):
    """
    Short final refinement using only peak subgradient.
    """
    p = _normalize_to_sum(np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64), np.sqrt(2.0 * len(p)))
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64), float("inf")

    S = np.sqrt(2.0 * float(n))
    b = _conv_fast(p)
    cur_score = _score_from_b(b, n, S)
    best = p.copy()
    best_score = cur_score

    m = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    t = 0
    lr_mult = 1.0
    fail = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 2.0:
        w = _make_weights(b, 0.0, 0.12)
        g = _gradient_from_weights(p, w)
        g = g - g.mean()
        gnorm = float(np.linalg.norm(g))
        if gnorm <= 1e-12:
            break

        dirn, m2, v2, t2 = _adam_direction(g, m, v, t)
        step = 0.0035 * lr_mult * (S / np.sqrt(float(n)))

        accepted = False
        last_cand = None
        last_cb = None
        last_cs = None

        for factor in (1.0, 0.5):
            cand = _project_sum(p - factor * step * dirn, S)
            cb = _conv_fast(cand)
            cs = _score_from_b(cb, n, S)
            if cs <= cur_score + 1e-12:
                p = cand
                b = cb
                cur_score = cs
                m = m2
                v = v2
                t = t2
                lr_mult = 1.0
                fail = 0
                accepted = True
                break
            else:
                last_cand = cand
                last_cb = cb
                last_cs = cs

        if not accepted:
            if fail >= 10 and last_cand is not None and last_cs < best_score * 1.2 + 0.2:
                p = last_cand
                b = last_cb
                cur_score = last_cs
                m = m2
                v = v2
                t = t2
                lr_mult = 1.0
                fail = 0
            else:
                lr_mult = max(0.2, lr_mult * 0.7)
                fail += 1
                if fail >= 25:
                    p = best.copy()
                    b = _conv_fast(p)
                    cur_score = best_score
                    m = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
                    v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
                    t = 0
                    lr_mult = 1.0
                    fail = 0

        if cur_score < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score = cur_score
            best = p.copy()

    best = _project_sum(best, S)
    return best, best_score


def _fast_candidates_if_no_time():
    """If time is extremely short, return a very good analytic candidate."""
    best_seq = None
    best_score = float("inf")

    # Fractional-power baseline.
    c = _harmononic_start(512)
    cS = np.sqrt(2.0 * 512)
    c = _normalize_to_sum(c, cS)
    sc = _score_fast(c)
    best_seq = c
    best_score = sc

    cr = _harmononic_start(512)[::-1].copy()
    cr = _normalize_to_sum(cr, cS)
    sr = _score_fast(cr)
    if sr < best_score:
        best_score = sr
        best_seq = cr

    # If previous global exists, maybe it is better.
    prev = _get_global_sequence()
    if prev is not None and prev.size > 0:
        pprev = _normalize_to_sum(prev, np.sqrt(2.0 * float(prev.size)))
        sprev = _score_fast(pprev)
        if sprev < best_score:
            best_score = sprev
            best_seq = pprev

    if best_seq is None:
        best_seq = np.ones(256, dtype=np.float64)

    out = _project_sum(best_seq, 1.0)
    return [float(x) for x in out]


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Main search function.
    Explores multiple lengths and starts, uses FFT gradient optimization,
    and optionally LP polish. Returns best non-negative sequence.
    """
    t_start = time.monotonic()

    try:
        budget = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget = 2400.0

    if not np.isfinite(budget) or budget <= 15.0:
        return _fast_candidates_if_no_time()

    total_span = max(30.0, budget - 15.0)
    deadline = t_start + total_span

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    prev = _get_global_sequence()

    best_seq = None
    best_score = float("inf")

    # Choose length schedule based on available time.
    if budget > 1000.0:
        lengths = [512, 1024, 2048, 4096]
        fracs = [0.10, 0.22, 0.34, 0.34]
    elif budget > 500.0:
        lengths = [512, 1024, 2048]
        fracs = [0.18, 0.34, 0.48]
    else:
        lengths = [512, 1024]
        fracs = [0.35, 0.65]

    current_start = prev
    cum = 0.0

    for ii, n in enumerate(lengths):
        rel_start = cum
        rel_end = cum + fracs[ii]
        cum = rel_end

        alloc_start = t_start + total_span * rel_start
        alloc_end = t_start + total_span * rel_end
        if ii == len(lengths) - 1:
            alloc_end = deadline

        if time.monotonic() > alloc_end - 25.0:
            break

        if current_start is not None and current_start.size > 0:
            base = _interpolate_sequence(current_start, n)
        else:
            base = _harmononic_start(n)

        starts = _make_starts(n, rng, base, prev)

        span = alloc_end - alloc_start
        if span >= 25.0:
            if len(starts) >= 1:
                dl1 = min(alloc_start + 0.75 * span, alloc_end - 10.0)
                seq1, score1 = _optimize_fft(starts[0], dl1, rng)
                if np.isfinite(score1) and score1 < best_score:
                    best_score = score1
                    best_seq = seq1
                print(f"length={n} start1 score={score1:.6f}")

                # If the first start improved the global best, use it as the
                # basis for the next length.
                if np.isfinite(score1) and (best_seq is None or score1 <= best_score + 1e-12):
                    current_start = seq1
                else:
                    if best_seq is not None:
                        current_start = best_seq

            if len(starts) >= 2 and time.monotonic() < alloc_end - 25.0:
                dl2 = alloc_end - 5.0
                seq2, score2 = _optimize_fft(starts[1], dl2, rng)
                if np.isfinite(score2) and score2 < best_score:
                    best_score = score2
                    best_seq = seq2
                print(f"length={n} start2 score={score2:.6f}")

                if np.isfinite(score2) and (best_seq is not None and score2 <= best_score + 1e-12):
                    current_start = seq2

        # For short lengths, apply a limited LP polish if time remains.
        if (
            best_seq is not None
            and len(best_seq) == n
            and n <= 512
            and time.monotonic() < alloc_end - 80.0
        ):
            seq_lp, score_lp = _lp_polish(best_seq, alloc_end - 10.0, max_iters=4, max_n=512)
            if np.isfinite(score_lp) and score_lp < best_score:
                best_score = score_lp
                best_seq = seq_lp
                print(f"lp polish length={n} score={score_lp:.6f}")

        # Keep the current best as the upscaling seed for the next larger length.
        if best_seq is not None:
            current_start = best_seq

    # If no sequence was found, fall back to a safe analytic start.
    if best_seq is None:
        best_seq = _harmononic_start(512)
        best_score = _score_fast(best_seq)

    # Final residual-time tail refinement on the best sequence found.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 15.0:
        seq_tail, score_tail = _tail_refine(best_seq, deadline - 2.0, rng)
        if np.isfinite(score_tail) and score_tail < best_score:
            best_score = score_tail
            best_seq = seq_tail
        print(f"tail refine score={score_tail:.6f}")

    # Return in unit-sum scaling. The objective is scale-invariant, and unit
    # sum avoids any risk from upper-clipping or the sum<0.01 guard.
    out = _project_sum(np.asarray(best_seq, dtype=np.float64), 1.0)

    # Final validation print (does not change returned sequence).
    try:
        ev = float(evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in out]))
        print(f"final internal best={best_score:.6f} evaluate_sequence={ev:.6f}")
    except Exception as exc:
        print(f"final internal best={best_score:.6f} evaluate_sequence unavailable/{exc}")

    return [float(x) for x in out]
```
